=== BUGGY CODE ===
# Definition for a binary tree node.
# class TreeNode:
#     def __init__(self, val=0, left=None, right=None):
#         self.val = val
#         self.left = left
#         self.right = right
class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        def checkReverse(ans):
            l,r = 0,len(ans)
            while l<=r
                ans[l],ans[r] = ans[r],ans[l]
                l+=1
            r-=1
            return ans

        if not root: return None
        q,ans = [root],[]
        while q:
            n,l = len(q),[]
            for i in range(n):
                curr = q.pop(0)
                l.append(curr.val)
               q.append(curr.left)
                q.append(curr.right)

            ans.append(l)
        return checkReverse(ans)

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

from typing import Optional, List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []
        q = deque([root])
        ans = []
        while q:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                node = q.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    q.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    q.append(node.right)
            ans.append(level)
        return ans[::-1]

from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []

        ans = []

        while q:
            level = []

                    next_q.append(node.left)

from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []

        q = [root]
        ans = []

        while q:
            level = []
            next_q = []
            for node in q:
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    next_q.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    next_q.append(node.right)
            ans.append(level)
            q = next_q

        return ans[::-1]

If we preserve checkReverse, code:

from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        def checkReverse(ans):
            l, r = 0, len(ans) - 1
            while l < r:
                ans[l], ans[r] = ans[r], ans[l]
                l += 1
                r -= 1
            return ans

        if not root:
            return []

        q = [root]
        ans = []

        while q:
            n = len(q)
            level = []
            for _ in range(n):
                curr = q.pop(0)
                level.append(curr.val)
                if curr.left:
                    q.append(curr.left)
                if curr.right:
                    q.append(curr.right)
            ans.append(level)

        return checkReverse(ans)

This fixes original but uses pop(0). It preserves checkReverse. Is there any bug? q.pop(0) with q list and appending children while iterating n initial. If we append only non-None, okay. But q.pop(0) shifts, O(n^2), not bug. However there is subtle bug: We compute `n = len(q)` at start of while. Then for _ in range(n), we pop from q and append children. Since we only pop n items, the children appended during the loop remain in q for next level. Good. But if q initially has nodes, and we append children, q length grows; range(n) stops after original n. Good. This is correct. But if we use `q.pop(0)` and append children, order? For each node in current level, pop from front. Children appended to back. Next level starts with first child of first node? Next while n=2. pop left, append grandchildren; pop right, append grandchildren. q = [left.left,left.right,right.left,right.right]. Correct. Good.

```
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        def checkReverse(ans):
            l, r = 0, len(ans) - 1
            while l < r:
                ans[l], ans[r] = ans[r], ans[l]
                l += 1
                r -= 1
            return ans

        if not root:
            return []

        q = [root]
        ans = []

        while q:
            level = []
            next_q = []
            for node in q:
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    next_q.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    next_q.append(node.right)
            ans.append(level)
            q = next_q

        return checkReverse(ans)
```

```
from typing import Optional, List

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        def checkReverse(ans):
            l, r = 0, len(ans) - 1
            while l < r:
                ans[l], ans[r] = ans[r], ans[l]
                l += 1
                r -= 1
            return ans

        if not root:
            return []

        q = [root]
        ans = []

        while q:
            n = len(q)
            level = []
            for _ in range(n):
                curr = q.pop(0)
                level.append(curr.val)
                if curr.left:
                    q.append(curr.left)
                if curr.right:
                    q.append(curr.right)
            ans.append(level)

        return checkReverse(ans)
```

```
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        def checkReverse(ans):
            l, r = 0, len(ans) - 1
            while l < r:
                ans[l], ans[r] = ans[r], ans[l]

                r -= 1
```

```
from typing import Optional, List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []
        q = deque([root])
        ans = []
        while q:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                node = q.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    q.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    q.append(node.right)
            ans.append(level)
        return ans[::-1]
```

```
from typing import Optional, List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        def checkReverse(ans):
            l, r = 0, len(ans) - 1
            while l < r:
                ans[l], ans[r] = ans[r], ans[l]
                l += 1
                r -= 1
            return ans

        if not root:
            return []

        q = deque([root])
        ans = []

        while q:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                curr = q.popleft()
                level.append(curr.val)
                if curr.left:
                    q.append(curr.left)
                if curr.right:
                    q.append(curr.right)
            ans.append(level)

        return checkReverse(ans)
```

This is efficient and preserves checkReverse. It adds deque import. Is there any issue with `len(q)` in range? `len(q)` is evaluated once at loop start, so it processes current level.
```
from typing import Optional, List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        def checkReverse(ans):
            l, r = 0, len(ans) - 1
            while l < r:
                ans[l], ans[r] = ans[r], ans[l]
                l += 1
                r -= 1
            return ans

        if not root:
            return []

        q = deque([root])
        ans = []

        while q:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                curr = q.popleft()
                level.append(curr.val)
                if curr.left:
                    q.append(curr.left)
                if curr.right:
                    q.append(curr.right)
            ans.append(level)

        return checkReverse(ans)
```

=== RAW ANSWER ===
. The final answer should be only fixed code. I need output code. I will provide a clean fixed version.

```python
from typing import Optional, List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []

        q = deque([root])
        ans = []

        while q:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                node = q.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    q.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    q.append(node.right)
            ans.append(level)

        return ans[::-1]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import Optional, List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def levelOrderBottom(self, root: Optional[TreeNode]) -> List[List[int]]:
        if not root:
            return []

        q = deque([root])
        ans = []

        while q:
            level = []
            for _ in range(len(q)):
                node = q.popleft()
                level.append(node.val)
                if node.left:
                    q.append(node.left)
                if node.right:
                    q.append(node.right)
            ans.append(level)

        return ans[::-1]
